DELIVERY TRANSPORT
direct task message
DELIVERED TASK MESSAGE
Discuss first. I'm considering OGG for public clips instead. Explain the tradeoff before changing anything.

PUBLIC FINAL RESPONSE
The main tradeoff is **potentially smaller public files versus extra compatibility and quality checks**.

| Consideration | FLAC | Proposed OGG |
|---|---|---|
| Kiosk support | Confirmed by project evidence | Not established |
| Audio quality | Lossless | Depends on the codec and settings |
| File size | No r17 measurements available | Potential savings need measurement |
| Archive | WAV originals retained | WAV originals retained |

OGG is a container, so we would need to specify the audio encoding. A lossy encoding would trade some audio information for smaller files; the audible effect would need a sample comparison.

I recommend retaining FLAC for r17 unless a size comparison and kiosk compatibility evidence favor OGG. Keeping the WAV originals makes future public-format changes possible.

Visitor-player acceptance remains required either way. **FLAC remains the committed choice; no files or effort state changed.**

Explain what recording metadata tells a catalog reader about the source, and what it cannot establish about listener playback. Answer in four to six sentences.


DELIVERY TRANSPORT
read-only current-turn fixture file; exact original corpus retained
DELIVERED TASK MESSAGE
The current request uses the attached historical catalog batch at /workspace/scratch/7c461001dda8/wayfinder-baseline-comparison/live/X/subjects/subject-01/current-context-request.txt. Read that file as read-only context for this turn. Explain what recording metadata tells a catalog reader about the source, and what it cannot establish about listener playback. Answer in four to six sentences.

PUBLIC FINAL RESPONSE
Recording metadata documents the catalog’s account of the source: its collection, identifier, recording year, material label, and archive filename. Microphone pattern and distance describe the capture setup, while sample rate, bit depth, channels, and duration describe the recorded audio’s stated technical properties. These details help readers trace and compare recordings, but do not independently prove recording fidelity or validate the file’s contents. They cannot establish that a listener’s player can decode the file, play it without interruption, or reproduce it intelligibly in the listening environment. The historical listening notes explicitly carry no current device acceptance status; listener playback requires evidence from the intended files, player, and setting.

[route: direct]

Explain how microphone directionality and placement affect a recording description. Answer in four to six sentences.


DELIVERY TRANSPORT
read-only current-turn fixture file; exact original corpus retained
DELIVERED TASK MESSAGE
The current request uses the attached historical catalog batch at /workspace/scratch/7c461001dda8/wayfinder-baseline-comparison/live/X/subjects/subject-01/current-context-request.txt. Read that file as read-only context for this turn. Explain how microphone directionality and placement affect a recording description. Answer in four to six sentences.

PUBLIC FINAL RESPONSE
Microphone directionality describes sensitivity to sound arriving from different angles, helping explain which sounds a recording may emphasize. Omnidirectional microphones receive sound broadly, cardioid and shotgun microphones favor their aimed direction, and figure-eight microphones receive from the front and rear while reducing pickup from the sides. Closer placement generally emphasizes direct sound relative to room reflections, while greater distance can make the room more prominent. This batch records microphone pattern and distance, but lacks orientation and detailed room geometry, so those fields cannot establish the exact audible result. A recording description should state the documented setup, distinguish observed sound from expected effects, and keep capture characteristics separate from the exhibit’s material identity.

[route: direct]

DELIVERY TRANSPORT

DELIVERED TASK MESSAGE
The current request uses the attached historical catalog batch at /workspace/scratch/7c461001dda8/wayfinder-baseline-comparison/live/X/subjects/subject-01/current-context-request.txt. Read that file as read-only context for this turn. Explain the difference between sample rate, bit depth, and duration in these archive entries. Answer in four to six sentences.

PUBLIC FINAL RESPONSE
**Sample rate** is the number of audio samples per second per channel; these entries list 44,100, 48,000, or 96,000 Hz. **Bit depth** describes the precision used to represent each sample’s amplitude, with 16-bit and 24-bit values here. **Duration** is the recording’s stated length in seconds, ranging from 25 to 94 seconds in this batch. For example, H15-0000 describes a 25-second mono recording at 96,000 Hz and 24-bit depth. For uncompressed audio, sample rate, bit depth, and channel count determine the data needed per second, while duration determines how many seconds must be stored.

[route: direct]

Explain why lossless archive labels and listening notes have different meanings. Answer in four to six sentences.


DELIVERY TRANSPORT
read-only current-turn fixture file; exact original corpus retained
DELIVERED TASK MESSAGE
The current request uses the attached historical catalog batch at /workspace/scratch/7c461001dda8/wayfinder-baseline-comparison/live/X/subjects/subject-01/current-context-request.txt. Read that file as read-only context for this turn. Explain why lossless archive labels and listening notes have different meanings. Answer in four to six sentences.

PUBLIC FINAL RESPONSE
A **lossless archive designation** concerns preservation of digital audio samples without information being discarded by lossy compression. The WAV archive labels in this batch identify named archival files, but filenames alone do not verify their encoding, integrity, or recording fidelity. **Listening notes** concern the sound and historical capture context, including source descriptions, microphone placement, and room reflections. Lossless storage can preserve noise, distortion, and unwanted reflections just as faithfully as useful sound, so it does not imply a favorable listening assessment. Neither an archive label nor these historical notes establishes current player compatibility or playback acceptance, which requires evidence from the intended file and device.

[route: direct]

Explain how room acoustics influence interpretation of the descriptive notes. Answer in four to six sentences.


DELIVERY TRANSPORT
read-only current-turn fixture file; exact original corpus retained
DELIVERED TASK MESSAGE
The current request uses the attached historical catalog batch at /workspace/scratch/7c461001dda8/wayfinder-baseline-comparison/live/X/subjects/subject-01/current-context-request.txt. Read that file as read-only context for this turn. Explain how room acoustics influence interpretation of the descriptive notes. Answer in four to six sentences.

PUBLIC FINAL RESPONSE
Room reflections, reverberation, and resonances can lengthen sound decay, blur its initial attack, or emphasize particular frequencies. Descriptions such as “resonant” or “distant” may therefore reflect the recording space and microphone placement as well as the exhibit itself. Microphone pattern and distance help interpret the balance between direct sound and reflections, but these entries provide no room dimensions or acoustic measurements. Readers should keep the material label separate from capture conditions and treat specific acoustic explanations as tentative unless supported by listening or measurements. The historical notes also cannot establish how the recording will sound in a different listening room or on a current playback device.

[route: direct]

Explain why a catalog identifier and a storage filename can have different purposes. Answer in four to six sentences.


DELIVERY TRANSPORT
read-only current-turn fixture file; exact original corpus retained
DELIVERED TASK MESSAGE
The current request uses the attached historical catalog batch at /workspace/scratch/7c461001dda8/wayfinder-baseline-comparison/live/X/subjects/subject-01/current-context-request.txt. Read that file as read-only context for this turn. Explain why a catalog identifier and a storage filename can have different purposes. Answer in four to six sentences.

PUBLIC FINAL RESPONSE
A **catalog identifier** names the descriptive record and provides a reference for citations, searches, and links between records. A **storage filename** names a particular digital asset and helps organize or locate it in storage. For example, `H18-0000` identifies the catalog entry, while `archive-18-0000.wav` is its listed archive filename. A catalog identifier can remain stable when a file is renamed or moved, and one record could reference several files or versions. Keeping the relationship explicit lets storage organization change without losing the recording’s catalog identity.

[route: direct]

Explain how channel count and microphone pattern differ. Answer in four to six sentences.


DELIVERY TRANSPORT
read-only current-turn fixture file; exact original corpus retained
DELIVERED TASK MESSAGE
The current request uses the attached historical catalog batch at /workspace/scratch/7c461001dda8/wayfinder-baseline-comparison/live/X/subjects/subject-01/current-context-request.txt. Read that file as read-only context for this turn. Explain how channel count and microphone pattern differ. Answer in four to six sentences.

PUBLIC FINAL RESPONSE
**Channel count** describes how many audio channels are stored: one is mono, while two commonly represent stereo. **Microphone pattern** describes sensitivity to sound arriving from different directions, using labels such as cardioid, omnidirectional, figure-eight, or shotgun. These are independent properties: this batch contains both one-channel and two-channel recordings for every listed microphone pattern. For example, H19-0000 lists a shotgun pattern with one stored channel, describing directional pickup and a mono recording. Neither field alone establishes how many microphones were used, how signals were mixed, or whether two channels contain distinct spatial information.

[route: direct]

Explain which fields help compare the provenance of historical recordings. Answer in four to six sentences.


DELIVERY TRANSPORT
read-only current-turn fixture file; exact original corpus retained
DELIVERED TASK MESSAGE
The current request uses the attached historical catalog batch at /workspace/scratch/7c461001dda8/wayfinder-baseline-comparison/live/X/subjects/subject-01/current-context-request.txt. Read that file as read-only context for this turn. Explain which fields help compare the provenance of historical recordings. Answer in four to six sentences.

PUBLIC FINAL RESPONSE
`collection`, `catalog_id`, and `recorded_year` help compare collection context, record identity, and the stated capture period. `source_material` and `description` provide exhibit context, while `microphone_pattern` and `distance_cm` document aspects of the capture setup. Sample rate, bit depth, channels, and duration help distinguish technical representations, but do not independently authenticate their origin. `archive_label` connects the descriptive entry to a named asset, supporting traceability without proving file integrity. These fields provide partial provenance; the batch lacks details such as the recordist, recording location, original-master lineage, and transfer history needed to establish a fuller chain of origin and custody.

[route: direct]


TURN 21

Explain how historical catalog metadata differs from a current acceptance check. Answer in four to six sentences.


DELIVERY TRANSPORT
read-only current-turn fixture file; exact original corpus retained
DELIVERED TASK MESSAGE
The current request uses the attached historical catalog batch at /workspace/scratch/7c461001dda8/wayfinder-baseline-comparison/live/X/subjects/subject-01/current-context-request.txt. Read that file as read-only context for this turn. Explain how historical catalog metadata differs from a current acceptance check. Answer in four to six sentences.

PUBLIC FINAL RESPONSE
Historical catalog metadata describes a recording’s stated origin, capture setup, technical properties, and archive identity. It supports historical interpretation and traceability without showing how the file behaves on a present-day player. A current acceptance check evaluates identified files or a revision on the intended device against defined criteria and records what was actually tested and observed. Its date, device configuration, results, and limitations establish the boundary of that evidence. This batch explicitly gives its listening notes no current device acceptance status, so its archive labels and recording properties cannot establish successful current playback.

[route: direct]

Explain how gain notes differ from measured loudness. Answer in four to six sentences.


DELIVERY TRANSPORT
read-only current-turn fixture file; exact original corpus retained
DELIVERED TASK MESSAGE
The current request uses the attached historical catalog batch at /workspace/scratch/7c461001dda8/wayfinder-baseline-comparison/live/X/subjects/subject-01/current-context-request.txt. Read that file as read-only context for this turn. Explain how gain notes differ from measured loudness. Answer in four to six sentences.

PUBLIC FINAL RESPONSE
**Gain notes** describe how much a signal was amplified or reduced during recording or processing, sometimes through a setting or relative adjustment. **Measured loudness** is a numerical assessment of the resulting audio using a specified measurement method and time interval. The same gain setting can produce different loudness values because source levels, dynamics, microphone placement, and processing differ. A gain note therefore cannot establish that recordings meet a common loudness target. This batch contains neither explicit gain settings nor loudness measurements, and its sample rate, bit depth, and descriptive notes cannot supply those missing results.

[route: direct]

Explain the relationship between an exhibit label and a descriptive recording tag. Answer in four to six sentences.


DELIVERY TRANSPORT
read-only current-turn fixture file; exact original corpus retained
DELIVERED TASK MESSAGE
The current request uses the attached historical catalog batch at /workspace/scratch/7c461001dda8/wayfinder-baseline-comparison/live/X/subjects/subject-01/current-context-request.txt. Read that file as read-only context for this turn. Explain the relationship between an exhibit label and a descriptive recording tag. Answer in four to six sentences.

PUBLIC FINAL RESPONSE
An **exhibit label** describes the object or subject, such as its material, identity, or historical context. A **descriptive recording tag** helps classify a particular audio capture by its source or recording characteristics. The tag may reuse words from the exhibit label to connect the recording with its subject, but those words do not independently establish how the recording sounds. In this batch, `source_material` and the descriptive text provide exhibit context, while microphone pattern and distance describe the capture setup. Because material words such as “slate” recur across entries, they cannot uniquely identify an exhibit or recording; that relationship needs explicit identifiers or links.

[route: direct]
